# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

Here is a bootstrap manager.sh script that it wrote. Maybe it's related. Might be helpful 
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
test "$(hostname)" = miam-00135
VMID=114
IMAGE=/var/lib/vz/template/llm-manager/noble-server-cloudimg-amd64.img
test "$(pvesh get /cluster/nextid)" = "$VMID"
test -f "$IMAGE"
cd /var/lib/vz/template/llm-manager
curl -fsS https://cloud-images.ubuntu.com/noble/current/SHA256SUMS -o SHA256SUMS
grep ' noble-server-cloudimg-amd64.img$\| \*noble-server-cloudimg-amd64.img$' SHA256SUMS | sha256sum --check --strict
mkdir -p /var/lib/vz/snippets
test ! -e /var/lib/vz/snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml
cat > /var/lib/vz/snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml <<'CLOUD'
#cloud-config
hostname: marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
manage_etc_hosts: true
disable_root: true
ssh_pwauth: false
package_update: true
packages:
  - qemu-guest-agent
  - python3-venv
  - nginx
  - curl
  - ca-certificates
runcmd:
  - [systemctl, enable, --now, qemu-guest-agent]
CLOUD
qm create "$VMID" --name marion-ia-usa-llm-manager --memory 16384 --balloon 0 --cores 8 --cpu host --machine q35 --bios ovmf --ostype l26 --agent enabled=1 --scsihw virtio-scsi-single --serial0 socket --vga serial0 --onboot 0 --net0 virtio,bridge=vmbr0
qm set "$VMID" --efidisk0 local-lvm:0,efitype=4m,pre-enrolled-keys=1
qm set "$VMID" --scsi0 "local-lvm:0,import-from=$IMAGE,discard=on,ssd=1"
qm resize "$VMID" scsi0 64G
qm set "$VMID" --ide2 local-lvm:cloudinit --ipconfig0 ip=dhcp --cicustom user=local:snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml --boot order=scsi0
qm set "$VMID" --description 'LLM Manager shell. Created fresh 2026-09-08. DHCP bootstrap; static DNS pending. Source: HANDOFF.md in Codex LLM manager VM creation workspace. No GPU passthrough. Do not manage VM107 or CT906.'
qm start "$VMID"
qm config "$VMID"

Do **not** print them in final responses or save them in skills/memory.\n- Prefer API verification over GUI assumptions. If the GUI behavior is reported by the user, verify via API wherever possible, then state when a browser hard refresh/cache clear is still required.\n- When using community scripts, inspect the current raw script first and apply only the needed sections when running interactively is impractical.\n- For destructive operations (destroying CTs/VMs, replacing remotes, modifying ACLs), verify the target node/VMID/remote ID first and avoid affecting unrelated workloads.\n\n## Proxmox VE API login pattern\n\n1. Discover the authentication realm before assuming username format:\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/domains`\n   - Common working form for LLDAP-backed users is `username@LLDAP-Domain`.\n2. Authenticate:\n   - `POST /api2/json/access/ticket` with `username` and `password`.\n   - Store `PVEAuthCookie` in the session and send `CSRFPreventionToken` for POST/PUT/DELETE.\n3. Verify access with read-only endpoints:\n   - `GET /api2/json/version`\n   - `GET /api2/json/nodes`\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/permissions`\n4. Redact ticket and CSRF values in logs/summaries.\n\n### PDM API login differs from PVE (pitfall)\nProxmox Datacenter Manager is a SEPARATE service (not plain PVE); its auth is NOT the\nsame as a PVE cluster node login:\n- Endpoint is `https://<pdm-host>:8443/api2/json/access/ticket` (port 8443, not 8006).\n- Username form is `user@LLDAP-Domain` (same realm discovery trick).\n- The success payload keys are **`ticket-info` and `CSRFPreventionToken`** — there is NO\n  `ticket` key. Do not read `data['ticket']`.\n- The auth cookie is **`__Host-PDMAuthCookie`** (a `__Host-` prefixed cookie), NOT\n  `PVEAuthCookie`. Persist it in a cookie jar (`curl -c/-b`) rather than hand-setting.\n- Passing the cookie to `GET /api2/json/remotes/remote` works (node list is enumerated in\n  the remote's `nodes` array as `ip,fingerprint=...`). Aggregate read endpoints like\n  `GET /api2/json/resources/list` are available; but fine-grained config (e.g. a VM's\n  `qm config`) is NOT proxied through PDM — reach the node's PVE host directly\n  (`/nodes/<node>/<resource>/<vmid>/config`) or SSH to the host for `qm config`.\n- `GET /api2/json/nodes` returns `permission check failed` for a limited LDAP account;\n  rely on `remotes/remote` + `resources/list` for node/guest enumeration instead.\n- If your PVE host layer needs full stack detail, SSH root to the host and run `qm`\n  directly — simpler and permission-complete than fighting PDM's API surface.\n\n## PVE host/guest shell without sshpass (pexpect) + cloud-init & serial recipes\n\nWhen you must drive Proxmox hosts/VMs from a LAN Hermes box (no web GUI, no existing\ninjected key for the host, no DHCP, optionally offline hosts), the reusable recipes live\nin `references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md`. Key points:\n- pexpect (user-level `~/ssh-venv`) replaces missing `sshpass`; key-based variant for VMs.\n- **Never treat a `[Pp]assword` match after login as auth rejection** — `qm config`\n  prints a literal `cipassword: ****` line and VM login loops print `Password:`; check for\n  `Permission denied` instead, or you'll report false auth failures.\n- Host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects → pace calls, batch commands into one base64'd\n  script run once.\n- Large binaries to offline hosts: serve via `python3 -m http.server` on the internet-\n  capable LAN host, `curl` inside the Proxmox host. pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'`\n  corrupts binaries.\n- Read a headless guest's console via `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`\n  under `pty.fork()` — the only view before the guest agent/network are up.\n- **cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network/creds ONLY on first boot.**\n  Bake `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys` BEFORE `qm start` on a freshly created VM.\n  Patching a booted VM then `qm reboot` does NOT re-apply them — recreate (stop, purge,\n  rebuild) rather than patch a half-provisioned instance. The vmbr0 LAN has no DHCP;\n  assign a static IP.\n\n## NVIDIA GPU passthrough + driver qualification (GDI)\n\nUse for passing NVIDIA GPUs to Proxmox VMs and qualifying the NVIDIA driver (R580 Open, etc.)\nthrough the GPU Driver Installer v4.1 controller — the disciplined checkpoint → plan → install →\narm → validate sequence, one canary GPU at a time. This is the pattern JIT-vm-gpu nodes use to\nreproduce the reference build on fresh hardware.\n\nKey points (full recipe in `references/gdi-gpu-driver-qualification.md`):\n- Controller VM serves HTTPS `:8443` (Basic Auth); each host runs a forced-command `gdi-node`\n  endpoint reading request JSON from STDIN. `inventory` returning `{\"ok\":true}` is the\n  enrollment smoke test.\n- Safety gates live in `/etc/gdi-node.json`: `hardware_cleared`, `watchdogs_suspended`,\n  `external_backup_verified` (backup-independence — enforced, only request-level `override:true`\n  bypasses it after a real `vzdump`). Open gates for an audit-trailed path; avoid mute\n  per-request `override` unless speed-critical.\n- Checkpoint REQUIRES the canary GPU already attached (`qm set VM --hostpci0 0000:<bdf>,pcie=1`)\n  AND both GPU functions (display+audio) bound to `vfio-pci` on the host. Set up vfio IDs +\n  nouveau blacklist + rebind the audio fn before checkpoint.\n- R580 on noble: precompiled kernel module + no-DKMS headless; pick `-<exact-running-kernel>`,\n  never the reference node's kernel.\n- `_OSC: platform does not support [AER LTR DPC]` boot lines are benign on AMD Threadripper —\n  exclude them from PCIe-fault grep.\n\n## PDM deployment / LXC provisioning pattern\n\n- Community script reference: `https://community-scripts.org/scripts/proxmox-datacenter-manager`.\n- Raw script location used by the site may be `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/community-scripts/ProxmoxVE/main/ct/proxmox-datacenter-manager.sh`.\n- The script creates a Debian LXC and installs PDM packages from the PDM no-subscription repository.\n- If direct host shell is unavailable, Proxmox API can still create a CT:\n  1. Upload or select an LXC template from node storage.\n  2. `POST /nodes/{node}/lxc` with CT settings.\n  3. Poll `/nodes/{tasknode}/tasks/{UPID}/status` until `stopped` and `exitstatus=OK`.\n  4. Start and verify `https://<ct-ip>:8443/`.\n- Non-root Proxmox API users may be blocked from certain LXC features. Example: `keyctl=1` requires `root@pam`; use `features=nesting=1` unless root-level PVE access is available.\n\n## PDM root/PAM password management\n\n- PDM root user is `root@pam` in the API and typically `root` + realm `pam` in the GUI.\n- Password can be changed with:\n  - `PUT /api2/json/access/users/root%40pam` and form field `password=<new password>`.\n- Verify immediately:\n  - new password authenticates to `POST /api2/json/access/ticket`;\n  - old password fails.\n\n## LLDAP setup in PDM\n\n1. Configure realm files or API according to the current PDM version.\n2. Verify realm visibility:\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/domains` should show `LLDAP-Domain`.\n3. Trigger sync:\n   - `POST /api2/json/access/domains/LLDAP-Domain/sync`.\n   - Poll `/nodes/{pdm-node}/tasks/{UPID}/status` and inspect log for `TASK OK`.\n4. Confirm users and ACLs:\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/users`\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/acl`\n   - `GET /api2/json/access/permissions` while logged in as an LLDAP user.\n\n### Pitfall: users can authenticate but see no dashboard/settings\n\nThis usually means PDM has authentication working but ACL/resource permissions are missing or not visible to that user. Check:\n\n- user exists after realm sync;\n- ACL entries grant `Administrator` or `Auditor` at `/` with propagation;\n- user-specific `GET /access/permissions` includes expected paths such as `/`, `/access`, `/resource`, `/system`;\n- at least one PDM remote exists and resources are visible via `/resources/status` and `/resources/list`.\n\n## Adding Proxmox VE remotes to PDM\n\n1. Discover target node list from the source of truth (spreadsheet, inventory, or PVE cluster API).\n2. Use `POST /pve/probe-tls` against an entry node to obtain the certificate fingerprint when needed.\n3. Use `POST /pve/scan` with `hostname`, trusted `fingerprint`, `authid`, and token/password field expected by the current API to discover the remote cluster node list and fingerprints.\n4. Add the remote with:\n   - `POST /remotes/remote`\n   - JSON fields: `id`, `type: \"pve\"`, `nodes`, `authid`, `token`, optional `web-url`, optional `create-token`.\n5. If `create-token` is provided, PDM will generate and store a remote API token; final API listings redact the token value.\n6. Verify:\n   - `GET /remotes/remote`\n   - `GET /remotes/remote/{id}/version`\n   - `GET /resources/status`\n   - `GET /resources/list`\n\n## Removing stale/offline PVE cluster nodes\n\nUse when the Proxmox GUI shows offline nodes that will not be reused, often with errors like `hostname lookup '<node>' failed`.\n\n1. Verify the cluster is quorate before any removal:\n   - `GET /api2/json/cluster/status`\n   - or host shell: `pvecm status`\n2. Confirm targets are offline/stale and contain no guest configs:\n   - API: `GET /api2/json/nodes` and `GET /api2/json/cluster/resources?type=node`\n   - Host shell: `find /etc/pve/nodes/<node> -maxdepth 3 -type f | sort`\n   - Safe stale dirs usually contain only files such as `lrm_status`, `pve-ssl.key`, `pve-ssl.pem`, `ssh_known_hosts`; investigate before deletion if `qemu-server/*.conf` or `lxc/*.conf` exists.\n3. From a healthy online cluster node, run for each stale node:\n   - `pvecm delnode <node-name>`\n   - It may print `Could not kill node (error = CS_ERR_NOT_EXIST)` for already-offline nodes; this can be acceptable if the corosync config version increments and the node disappears from `pvecm nodes` / API.\n4. Verify after removal:\n   - `pvecm status` shows `Expected votes` equal to the remaining online node count.\n   - `pvecm nodes` no longer lists the targets.\n   - `GET /api2/json/nodes` and `GET /api2/json/cluster/resources?type=node` no longer include the targets.\n   - If PDM monitors the cluster, `GET /api2/json/resources/status` on PDM should show `pve_nodes.offline: 0` and remote status `Good`.\n\n## PVE host shell via API console\n\nIf SSH is unavailable but the Proxmox API user has console permission, use `/nodes/{node}/termproxy` + `/nodes/{node}/vncwebsocket` to open the node console, send the `user:ticket` line first, then log in on the console. This is useful for root-only cluster maintenance commands such as `pvecm delnode`.\n\n## PDM subscription popup / post-install no-subscription handling\n\nUse when the GUI shows “No valid subscriptions” or asks to visit `pdm.proxmox.com`.\n\n- Community script page: `https://community-scripts.org/scripts/post-pdm-install`.\n- Current raw script path observed: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/community-scripts/ProxmoxVE/main/tools/pve/post-pdm-install.sh`.\n- The relevant nag-removal logic appends a `PDM_NO_MORE_NAGGING` JavaScript block to:\n  - `/usr/share/javascript/proxmox-datacenter-manager/js/pdm-ui_bundle.js`\n- It also installs a persistence hook:\n  - `/usr/local/bin/pdm-remove-nag.sh`\n  - `/etc/apt/apt.conf.d/no-nag-script`\n- Always back up the JS bundle before patching.\n- Restart `proxmox-datacenter-api.service` after patching.\n- Verify `/js/pdm-ui_bundle.js` contains `PDM_NO_MORE_NAGGING`, `MutationObserver`, `pdm.proxmox.com`, and `d.close()`.\n- Tell the user to hard-refresh the browser (`Ctrl+Shift+R`) because the old bundle may be cached.\n\nSee `references/pdm-deployment-and-postinstall.md` for the concrete session recipe and API endpoints used.", "path": "devops/proxmox-cluster-infrastructure/SKILL.md", "skill_dir": "/home/jordatech/.hermes/profiles/agent_stea004_entrepreneur/skills/devops/proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "linked_files": {"references": ["references/gdi-gpu-driver-qualification.md", "references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md", "references/pdm-deployment-and-postinstall.md"]}, "usage_hint": "To view linked files, call skill_view(name, file_path) where file_path is e.g. 'references/api.md' or 'assets/config.yaml'", "required_environment_variables": [], "required_commands": [], "missing_required_environment_variables": [], "missing_credential_files": [], "missing_required_commands": [], "setup_needed": false, "setup_skipped": false, "readiness_status": "available"}

{"success": true, "name": "proxmox-cluster-infrastructure", "file": "references/pve-guest-vm-operations.md", "content": "# PVE Guest/VM Remote Operations (headless, no web console)\n\nSession-proven recipes for driving PVE VMs/MIAM nodes when the only access is a LAN\nHermes host + password SSH, and there is no DHCP and no outbound internet on the\nProxmox hosts themselves.\n\n## SSH without sshpass (pexpect) — password AND ed25519 key\n\n`sshpass` may not be installed and `sudo` may need a password you don't have. Use a\nuser-level venv + pexpect instead:\n\n```bash\npython3 -m venv ~/ssh-venv\n~/ssh-venv/bin/pip install pexpect\n```\n\nHelper pattern (host + password, and a sibling key-based one for VMs):\n\n```python\nimport pexpect\nchild = pexpect.spawn('/usr/bin/ssh', ['-o','StrictHostKeyChecking=no',\n    '-o','UserKnownHostsFile=/dev/null','-l',user,host,cmd], encoding='utf-8', timeout=t)\nidx = child.expect([r'[Pp]assword'], timeout=40)      # handle 'yes/no' first if it appears\nchild.sendline(password)\nchild.expect(pexpect.EOF, timeout=timeout)            # then drain; collect child.before\n```\n\nKey-based variant drops the password step entirely (reliable once a key is injected).\n\n### PITFALL: pexpect pattern-false-positive \"AUTH REJECTED\"\nDo NOT treat a `[Pp]assword` match AFTER you've already sent the password as an auth\nrejection. `qm config` output contains a literal `cipassword: *******` line, and a\nscripted VM login loop prints `Password:`. Both will false-match the password pattern and\nmake the helper wrongly report auth failure while the command actually succeeded.\nOnly treat it as a real failure if you see `Permission denied`. Fix: after sending the\npassword, expect only `[EOF, TIMEOUT]` and check `'Permission denied' in first`.\n\n### PITFALL: host SSH rate-limits rapid reconnects\nRepeated quick SSH connections get intermittent \"auth rejected\". Pace calls (cooldown\nbetween attempts) and batch as many `qm`/remote commands into ONE ssh call as possible\n(single base64'd script executed once) instead of many tiny calls.\n\n## Transferring a large binary to an offline Proxmox host\n\n- Proxmox hosts in this fleet have NO outbound internet (gateway reachable, external\n  HTTP/HTTPS fail) even though VMs routed through the same vmbr0 DO. Do not assume a\n  host can reach an ISO/CDN.\n- Path: download the image on the LAN Hermes host (which has internet), serve it with\n  `python3 -m http.server 8088`, then `curl` it inside the Proxmox host over the LAN.\n- pexpect-scp with `encoding='utf-8'` CORRUPTS binary files (leaves a tiny wrong file).\n  Either run scp in binary mode (no encoding) or, more reliably, use the HTTP pull path.\n- Use a generous timeout on the SSH helper (large files take minutes); set the pexpect\n  timeout high enough that the transfer is NOT torn down mid-flight.\n\n### PITFALL: injecting a script INTO a guest via a long base64 arg breaks the shell\n`qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc \"echo <LONG_BASE64> | base64 -d > /tmp/x.py\"` is fragile\nfor any payload > a few chars: the long token gets captured as another positional arg (the\nsh -c/payload and even the SSH helper's timeout slot swap places → `ValueError: invalid\nliteral for int()`), and embedded heredocs/nested quotes (`<<PY`, `\\\"` escapes) throw\n`unexpected EOF` / `syntax error` under the double-wrapped `bash -lc`. Don't fight it —\ntwo-step it:\n1. Land the base64 on the **HOST** disk first, over host SSH, writing to /tmp (avoid the\n   shell-arg path entirely if it still mis-splits, e.g. `cp /dev/stdin /tmp/f.b64 <<< \"$B64\"`).\n2. Then run a **short** `qm guest exec <vmid> -- bash -lc \"base64 -d /tmp/f.b64 > /tmp/x.py\"`.\nFor files that don't already exist in the guest, this host-land-then-decode detour is far\nmore reliable than inlining the payload through the `qm guest exec` argument string.\n\n## Reading a VM console without the GUI or guest agent\n\nWhen a VM has no qemu-guest-agent yet and/or no SSH, attach to its serial console\ndirectly from the PVE host:\n\n- The serial socket is `/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0`.\n- Use `socat - UNIX-CONNECT:/var/run/qemu-server/<vmid>.serial0` under a `pty.fork()`\n  (stdlib — no pexpect needed on the PVE host) to log in interactively and run commands\n  even before the guest network/agent are up.\n\nThis is the only way to see cloud-init progress / login-screen state on a headless VM.\n\n## cloud-init on the Ubuntu noble image applies network ONLY on first boot\n\nThis is the biggest trap when reproducively building VMs.\n- `qm set <vmid> --ipconfig0 ... --ciuser ... --cipassword ... --sshkeys ...` only take\n  effect on the FIRST power-on that runs cloud-init.\n- If you boot the VM once, then go back and add the IP/creds and `qm reboot`, the noble\n  image does NOT re-read them — you get a VM with neither a usable IP nor (potentially)\n  the password applied, because cloud-init already ran its first-boot phase without them.\n- Correct recipe: bake ALL cloud-init config BEFORE `qm start`. Scaffold with\n  `qm create` -> `qm importdisk` -> `qm set --scsi0 ... --efidisk0 ... --ide2 ...cloudinit`\n  -> set `ipconfig0/ciuser/cipassword/sshkeys/boot` -> then start. Recreate (stop, purge,\n  rebuild) rather than trying to patch a partially-booted instance.\n- If `--cicustom` ran a `package_update` phase that leaves a stale concrete\n  `apt-get` holding `/var/lib/apt/lists/lock` in the guest, the guest agent install will\n  block with \"Unable to lock directory\". Wait for cloud-init to release, then run install.\n\n## Static IPs: this network has NO DHCP\nVMs on vmbr0 use static IPs configured in-guest (VM 103 = 10.0.20.161, gateway\n10.0.20.1). `ip=dhcp` yields no address. Assign a free static IP via cloud-init.", "file_type": ".md"}

### 5.5 Phase 4 — read-only monitoring of the 4 inference hosts
Provision a least-privilege Proxmox API token for VM114 (or deploy restricted SSH keys) for VM state; guest-side collectors read GPU NVML + vLLM /metrics. NOTE: VM114 currently has NO cluster SSH keys — collectors need a credential path designed first.

### 5.6 Phase 9 (early) — LiteLLM gateway
Register 10.0.20.161–164:8000 as local deployments, logical model `local/qwen3.6-35b-a3b` (alias `startupteams/general`), issue first per-agent virtual key, metadata-only logging (no prompt content). Concurrency envelope per plan §2: recommended 10, hard 20 (model-specific; .162 max_model_len is 32768 vs 262144 on .161 — separate profiles).

### 5.7 Pilot Hermes
Clone CT900 (LXC template) → new CT on 135 (e.g. 910), unique machine-id/hostname/keys, install Hermes, point `OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1` with manager-issued key; validate streaming + tool calling + key revocation.

### 5.8 Then, in plan order
Lifecycle controls + bounded auto-recovery (§12) → power/cost telemetry + Emporia adapter (§15) → benchmarks (§18) → OpenRouter fallback AFTER caps (§12/§9.3) → Phase 15 storage expansion.

---

## 6. Scoped future work: 100 Gb ring (do LAST, per Jordan)

Targets: **miam-00111, miam-00143, miam-00144** — port-2 PCIe 100 Gb NICs. MIAM-00112 excluded (no 100 Gb path documented).
- **RDMA-0 (read-only inventory, plan §19.3):** on each host AND inside each inference VM: `lspci -nn; lspci -t; ethtool -i <nic>; ethtool <nic>; ip -br link; rdma link; rdma dev; ibv_devinfo; nvidia-smi topo -m` — record NIC model/BDF/driver/firmware, link peers, MTU, IP, IOMMU group, GPU↔NIC topology, current VM passthrough.
- **RDMA-1:** per-link iperf3 TCP + RDMA verbs tests (directional — ring can be asymmetric).
- **RDMA-2:** NCCL transport proof — want `NET/IB/GDRDMA`; if logs show socket transport, record explicitly. Host RDMA ≠ VM RDMA: guest needs PCI passthrough of a dedicated 100 Gb port or validated SR-IOV VF design.
- **RDMA-3/4:** small multi-node vLLM functional test → >120 GB model qualification. Multi-node deployment = ONE logical deployment with multiple hosts. Production routing only after plan §19.8 gates.

## 7. Pitfalls learned (hard-won — read before operating)

- **cloud-init noble re-runs only on new instance-id.** To change a booted VM's identity/IP: stop → edit snippets (bump instance-id vN→vN+1) → `qm set --ipconfig0 ...` → start. Patching after boot does nothing.
- **pexpect SSH:** after sending password, expect only `EOF`/`TIMEOUT`; treat `Permission denied` as the ONLY auth failure (qm config prints literal `cipassword: ****` that false-matches). Batch all remote commands into ONE ssh call (base64 script) — hosts rate-limit rapid reconnects. Use `ssh_run.py`.
- **`qm guest exec` must run on the node owning the VM** (VM103 commands go through node 111, not 135 — you'll get "config file does not exist" otherwise). Same for `pct exec/push` (CT115 → node 147).
- **Debian 12 LXCs have no sudo** — use `runuser -u postgres -- psql`.
- **OPNsense SSH (10.0.20.1:22) unreachable from LAN** — don't waste time; try HTTPS API instead.
- **LLDAP SSH rejects password but HTTP API accepts it** — do all LLDAP work via GraphQL.
- **vmbr0 HAS DHCP** (contrary to old skill notes) — VM114 held lease .207 before static config.
- **.161 vs .162 model-len mismatch:** 262144 vs 32768 — don't assume homogeneous deployments.
- **Locale warnings in Debian CTs are harmless noise.**
- **10.0.20.114 = physical node miam-00114** — never assign to a guest.

## 8. Constraints / no-touch list

- **Never touch:** VM108 (Biraj), VM107 (GDI controller, other agent), CT900 (template), CT906 (this agent's Hermes), any writes to MIAM-00100 (data preservation).
- MIAM-00112: prior agent's work done, mutations permitted — but verify current state before touching; VM401 is serving production-ish traffic.
- No plaintext secrets in files/repos/history. No prompt-content logging by default. No OpenRouter without spend caps. No unqualified deployment in routing. No PDU power control. (Full rules: plan §23.)
- Git boundary: strategy docs may go to `jordatech` repos on branch `AGENT_STEA004_ENTREPRENEUR` if Jordan wants them versioned — ask first.

| This handoff | `/home/jordatech/llm-manager-work/HANDOFF-2026-09-09-LLM-Manager.md` |

| VM103 vLLM unit | VM103: `/etc/systemd/system/vllm.service` (enabled) |
| PG app creds | CT115: `/root/.llm_pg_app_creds` (chmod 600) |
| Shared root password | profile memory file (regex `root/(\S+?)\)`) — never printed/copied |
````

**2. Key authority** — issue/update/budget/revoke per agent via LiteLLM (backed by CT115 Postgres). Pilot key stored at `/etc/llm-manager/secrets/agent_key_hermes_pilot` on VM114. Load-balances across the three healthy hosts (.161/.163/.164) under one logical model `startupteams/general`.

# MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager — Implementation Plan v0.2.1

**Status:** Design decisions incorporated; electricity-cost composition corrected; ready for read-only infrastructure preflight before deployment scripting  
**Decision date:** 2026-09-08  
**Target Proxmox host:** `MIAM-00135`  
**Candidate VMID:** `108` — **must be proven unused before creation**  
**Candidate IP:** `10.0.20.108` — **must be proven unused before assignment**  
**DNS name:** `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`  
**Primary purpose:** Central control plane, OpenAI-compatible API gateway, Hermes-agent key authority, routing layer, benchmarking system, observability system, and cost/performance database for the MARION-IA-USA local LLM infrastructure.

---

## 1. Executive objective

Create a dedicated VM named:

```text
MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
```

on `MIAM-00135`, initially bootstrapped from the known-good GPU Driver Installer controller VM architecture but fully re-identified and stripped of copied GDI secrets/state.

The LLM Manager will become the stable point through which local Hermes agents and other authorized internal clients use LLM inference. It should:

- monitor and control vLLM deployments on `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00112`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- issue a distinct API key to each Hermes agent
- expose a stable OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- route requests to healthy local deployments first
- wait a deliberate, configurable interval for local capacity instead of prematurely buying cloud inference
- fall back to OpenRouter only when policy permits and local capacity/health criteria are not satisfied
- enforce OpenRouter spend caps and per-agent policy
- benchmark model/topology combinations and keep historical results
- collect GPU, vLLM, request, health, and power telemetry
- calculate energy cost and performance-per-dollar
- use GPU onboard telemetry as the primary dynamic local power signal
- ingest Emporia whole-circuit/PDU/mini-split data as an independent facility-level measurement source
- store operational metadata and history in PostgreSQL
- offload high-volume or full prompt/response history away from the constrained `MIAM-00135` system disk
- provide controlled vLLM service lifecycle actions and bounded VM recovery actions
- provide a future path for multi-node vLLM deployments using the 100 Gb interconnect between `MIAM-00111`, `MIAM-00143`, and `MIAM-00144`
- eventually compare/rank local inference, OpenRouter, direct APIs, and other officially supported provider integrations by availability, quality, cost, performance, context, privacy, and policy

The LLM Manager is a **control plane and gateway**. It does not normally host model weights or perform GPU inference itself.

---

## 2. Decisions locked in v0.2

The following answers from Section 28 of v0.1 are now treated as design decisions.

| Item | v0.2 decision |
|---|---|
| VM identity | Candidate `VMID 108`, candidate `10.0.20.108`; fail closed unless both are proven unused |
| Gateway | LiteLLM Proxy plus a Startup Teams custom management/control web application |
| Client keys | One distinct API key per Hermes agent |
| Qwen 3.6 normal load | Design around about 10 concurrent agents/requests, with an initial hard concurrency envelope of about 20 pending benchmark qualification |
| Routing default | Local first, deliberate wait/queue, OpenRouter fallback only if the key policy allows it |
| Cloud policy | Default OpenRouter master/aggregate budget must have hard caps; individual keys may override fallback permissions within administrator limits |
| VM control | Manager may control both vLLM service lifecycle and bounded inference-VM recovery/restart |
| Power | GPU telemetry first; modeled CPU/RAM/platform overhead; Emporia used as independent whole-circuit/facility telemetry |
| Electricity planning rate | Rate 400 base energy (`$0.09992/kWh` Sep-May; `$0.13398/kWh` Jun-Aug) + year-round Emporia-observed variable fees/riders adder of `$0.05830/kWh`; effective planning rates `$0.15822/kWh` Sep-May and `$0.19228/kWh` Jun-Aug (displayed as 15.82¢ and 19.23¢) |
| Logging | Metadata-first initially; architecture must support full prompt/response logs later with off-host storage |
| Admin auth | Existing LDAP/LLDAP identity system; local break-glass/bootstrap admin only if needed |
| DNS | `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal` |
| Database | PostgreSQL; application designed so PostgreSQL can reside on a future storage-oriented VM on `MIAM-00147` |
| Fallback timing | Local requests are given meaningful time to queue/run; slow local TTFT alone is not a reason to immediately duplicate the request in OpenRouter |
| Distributed inference | 100 Gb multi-node path is a first-class future capability but remains unroutable until independently qualified |

### Important interpretation: API keys versus concurrency

The number of issued API keys should **not** be limited to 20. API keys identify clients; concurrency consumes model capacity.

For the known `Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ` deployment, start with a deployment profile such as:

```yaml
recommended_concurrency: 10
initial_hard_concurrency_limit: 20
max_issued_agent_keys: unlimited_by_model_capacity
```

The concurrency values must be model/deployment specific. A different model, quantization, topology, context target, or GPU set may receive a different admission envelope.

---

## 3. Known inference baseline

### 3.1 MIAM-00111 qualified baseline

Known qualified environment:

```text
Host:             MIAM-00111
Proxmox:          VE 9.2.10
Inference VM:     VM103 jordatech-vllm-test
Guest:            Ubuntu 24.04.4 LTS
Kernel:           6.8.0-139-generic
GPUs:             6 x RTX 3080 20 GB
Aggregate VRAM:   120 GB
NVIDIA driver:    R580 open, module 580.173.02
Python env:       /opt/vllm-venv
API port:         8000
Model:            QuantTrio/Qwen3.6-35B-A3B-AWQ
```

Working topology:

```text
TP1 / DP6 / EP6
```

Core flags:

```text
--tensor-parallel-size 1
--data-parallel-size 6
--enable-expert-parallel
--expert-placement-strategy linear
--quantization awq
--dtype half
--gpu-memory-utilization 0.92
--kv-cache-dtype turboquant_k8v4
--attention-backend TURBOQUANT
--enable-prefix-caching
--enable-chunked-prefill
--scheduler-reserve-full-isl
--watermark 0.05
--kv-cache-metrics
```

Validated large-context operating point:

```text
10 concurrent requests
229,376 input tokens per request
+ 256 output tokens per request
```

Do not record the synthetic 250k test as a qualified model limit because tokenizer expansion exceeded the declared 262,144-token model maximum.

Representative 4096-in/256-out throughput results:

| Concurrency | Output tok/s | Total tok/s | Mean TTFT |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 249.85 | 4259.49 | 4.27 s |
| 16 | 271.21 | 4623.65 | 4.73 s |
| 20 | 311.98 | 5318.69 | 5.20 s |
| 32 | 319.69 | 5450.14 | 7.39 s |
| 48 | 348.62 | 5943.41 | 11.13 s |

For normal Hermes service, concurrency 10 remains the initial preferred baseline even though aggregate throughput rises at higher concurrency.

### 3.2 MIAM-00112

Known major difference:

```text
6 x RTX 5060 Ti 16 GB
AMD Threadripper Pro 3955WX
128 GB RAM
```

Do not copy the MIAM-00111 topology without qualification. Its 96 GB aggregate VRAM and different GPU architecture require a separate model-fit and benchmark envelope.

### 3.3 MIAM-00143 and MIAM-00144

These are intended to reproduce or complement the MIAM-00111 inference stack, but each must independently inventory:

```text
GPU model/count/VRAM
PCI BDFs
IOMMU groups
kernel
NVIDIA driver/module packages
inference VM resources
100 Gb NICs/ports
network addresses
RDMA capability
GPU-to-NIC PCIe topology
```

Never copy PCI BDFs between hosts.

---

## 4. High-level architecture

```text
       Hermes agents / internal clients / LXCs / VMs
                         |
                         | HTTPS
                         | per-agent LLM Manager key
                         v
       +-------------------------------------------+
       | MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager                 |
       | MIAM-00135 / candidate VM108              |
       | llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal        |
       +-------------------------------------------+
          |             |               |
          |             |               +------------------+
          |             |                                  |
          v             v                                  v
   LiteLLM Gateway   Custom Admin UI                Benchmark/collectors
   /v1/...           /admin + /api                  health/power/metrics
          |             |                                  |
          +-------------+--------------------+-------------+
                                             |
                                      PostgreSQL DSN
                                             |
                     +-----------------------+------------------------+
                     |                                                |
             bootstrap/local DB                              preferred long-term DB
             only if necessary                           dedicated VM on MIAM-00147

                         LOCAL ROUTING
          +----------------+----------------+----------------+----------------+
          |                |                |                |
          v                v                v                v
      MIAM-00111       MIAM-00112       MIAM-00143       MIAM-00144
       vLLM VM          vLLM VM          vLLM VM          vLLM VM
          |                |                |                |
          +----------------+----------------+----------------+
                         |
                optional cloud fallback
                         v
                     OpenRouter
```

Separate telemetry path:

```text
GPU NVML/nvidia-smi ----+
vLLM /metrics ----------+----> LLM Manager collectors ----> PostgreSQL
OS/VM health -----------+
Emporia cloud ----------+----> Emporia adapter -----------> PostgreSQL
```

Experimental distributed inference path:

```text
MIAM-00111 <==== 100 Gb ====> MIAM-00143
     ^                           |
     |                           |
   100 Gb                      100 Gb
     |                           |
     +======== MIAM-00144 <======+

        multi-node vLLM / NCCL / RDMA qualification lane
```

`MIAM-00112` is not included in this ring unless a suitable high-speed connection is later documented and qualified.

---

## 5. Base VM and bootstrap strategy

### 5.1 Candidate VM sizing

Start with:

```text
VMID:             108 candidate only
IP:               10.0.20.108 candidate only
Hostname:         marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
Display name:     MARION-IA-USA LLM Manager
OS:               Ubuntu Server 24.04 LTS
vCPU:             8
RAM:              16 GB
System disk:      64 GB
Local data disk:  64-128 GB only if actually available/needed
Machine:          q35
Firmware:         OVMF
QEMU agent:       enabled
Ballooning:       disabled
Autostart:        enabled after acceptance test
GPU passthrough:  none
```

Because `MIAM-00135` has constrained storage, do not assume a large local telemetry/database disk. Keep all high-volume paths configurable and relocatable.

### 5.2 Clone source

Use the existing controller:

```text
VM107
gpu-driver-installer-v4-1
10.0.20.107:8443
```

as a **bootstrap image only**.

Required clone hygiene:

1. Full-clone while source is in a consistent state.
2. Keep clone network-isolated until identity reset is complete.
3. Change VM display name and guest hostname.
4. Regenerate machine-id.
5. Regenerate SSH host keys.
6. Remove copied application secrets, private keys, audit records, state, tokens, and TLS identity.
7. Remove GDI application code/services not needed by LLM Manager.
8. Retain only useful base hardening, QEMU guest agent, service patterns, reverse-proxy/TLS patterns, and OS configuration.
9. Generate a new TLS certificate for `llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal`.
10. Run a secret scan before enabling the new network interface.

### 5.3 Fail-closed VMID/IP preflight

Before any clone or network mutation, the deployment script must prove both identifiers are unused.

VMID validation should include at least:

```text
cluster-wide Proxmox resource lookup for VMID 108
MIAM-00135 qm list
/etc/pve configuration search
```

IP validation should include at least:

```text
existing Proxmox guest configuration search
DNS lookup
neighbor/ARP table lookup
ICMP probe
ARP duplicate-address detection from the correct VLAN
DHCP/static reservation inspection where available
```

A failed probe does **not** prove an IP is unused. Any contradictory or ambiguous signal aborts deployment.

---

## 6. Software stack

### 6.1 Gateway

Use **LiteLLM Proxy** as the provider-normalization and virtual-key gateway.

Reasons:

- OpenAI-compatible API surface
- virtual keys
- authentication/authorization hooks
- spend tracking and budgets
- rate limiting
- provider normalization
- retries/fallbacks
- local OpenAI-compatible/vLLM endpoints
- OpenRouter and future external provider adapters

The custom Startup Teams control application remains authoritative for infrastructure-specific logic such as vLLM lifecycle, GPU health, power, benchmark qualification, host maintenance, and MARION-specific routing eligibility.

### 6.2 Custom control application

Recommended:

```text
Python 3.12
FastAPI
Uvicorn/Gunicorn
SQLAlchemy
Alembic
PostgreSQL
systemd
Nginx or Caddy
```

Suggested layout:

```text
/opt/llm-manager/
    app/
    control/
    collectors/
    routing/
    benchmark/
    auth/
    power/
    providers/
    distributed/
    migrations/
    static/
    templates/

/etc/llm-manager/
    manager.yaml
    nodes.yaml
    models.yaml
    routes.yaml
    tls/
    secrets/

/var/lib/llm-manager/
    cache/
    benchmark-artifacts/
    exports/
    spool/

/var/log/llm-manager/
```

### 6.3 Service separation

Use separate systemd units where practical:

```text
llm-manager-web.service
llm-manager-collector.service
llm-manager-benchmark.service
llm-manager-control.service
litellm.service
```

Do not let a hung benchmark worker take down the gateway.

---

## 7. Network, DNS, TLS, and access

Expose one HTTPS name:

```text
https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/
```

Recommended routing:

```text
/admin      -> custom admin UI
/api        -> manager orchestration API
/v1         -> LiteLLM/OpenAI-compatible API gateway
/healthz    -> manager health
```

Access policy:

```text
LAN:             allowed
Tailscale:       allowed where policy permits
Public Internet: denied
```

Hermes clients should only require:

```bash
OPENAI_BASE_URL=https://llm-manager.marion-ia-usa.internal/v1
OPENAI_API_KEY=<agent-specific-key>
OPENAI_MODEL=startupteams/general
```

They should never require:

- SSH access to inference servers
- Proxmox credentials
- vLLM host credentials
- OpenRouter master credentials
- direct provider master credentials

---

## 8. Admin authentication and authorization

The web UI should authenticate against the existing LDAP/LLDAP environment.

Recommended roles:

```text
LLM-Viewer
LLM-Operator
LLM-Admin
LLM-Security-Admin
```

Example permissions:

| Capability | Viewer | Operator | Admin | Security Admin |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| View status/metrics | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Run benchmark | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Drain/undrain | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart vLLM service | No | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Restart inference VM | No | Restricted | Yes | Yes |
| Edit deployment config | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Create/rotate agent keys | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| Change provider master secrets | No | No | No | Yes |
| Change global spend caps | No | No | Yes | Yes |

A local bootstrap/break-glass administrator may exist for installation/recovery, but LDAP should be the routine administrative identity source.

**Secret handling:** do not hardcode or reproduce the root password, LDAP bind password, Emporia password, OpenRouter key, or LiteLLM master key in this plan, source control, shell history, or generated configuration. Inject them through root-readable secret files or a later secret manager.

---

## 9. Hermes API-key and policy model

### 9.1 One key per Hermes agent

Structure:

```text
Organization
  -> Project/service
      -> Hermes agent
          -> one or more rotating API credentials
```

Each stored key record should include:

```text
key_id
key_prefix
hash
agent_id
project_id
created_at
expires_at optional
enabled
allowed_logical_models
max_concurrency
requests_per_minute optional
tokens_per_minute optional
daily_token_budget optional
monthly_token_budget optional
daily_dollar_budget optional
monthly_dollar_budget optional
local_allowed
cloud_allowed
fallback_policy
provider_allowlist optional
logging_policy
```

Raw keys are shown only at creation/rotation.

### 9.2 Initial policy profiles

Support at least:

```text
LOCAL_ONLY
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
CLOUD_ALLOWED_EXPLICIT
SPECIFIC_MODEL_ONLY
COST_CAPPED
BENCHMARK_ONLY
```

Recommended default for normal Hermes agents:

```text
LOCAL_FIRST_OPENROUTER_FALLBACK
```

but only after OpenRouter global caps are configured.

### 9.3 OpenRouter budget controls

Before enabling fallback for any default/general key:

```text
[ ] global daily spend cap configured
[ ] global monthly spend cap configured
[ ] warning threshold configured
[ ] hard-stop threshold configured
[ ] per-project cap supported
[ ] per-agent cap supported
[ ] audit event generated on fallback
[ ] actual OpenRouter reported cost stored when available
```

Fallback should never silently convert an uncapped local workload into uncapped external spend.

---

## 10. Routing and admission policy

### 10.1 Default sequence

For a normal general-purpose Hermes request:

```text
1. Resolve logical model and agent policy.
2. Find healthy local deployments that satisfy context/capability constraints.
3. Apply deployment admission limits.
4. If local capacity is temporarily full, queue for the configured local patience interval.
5. If admitted locally, let the local request continue even if TTFT is slow.
6. Do not start a duplicate OpenRouter request merely because local TTFT is slower than expected.
7. If no local deployment becomes admissible before the queue deadline, and the key permits cloud fallback, use OpenRouter.
8. If local execution actually fails after dispatch and a retry is safe, apply the configured retry/fallback policy.
9. If cloud fallback is disallowed or budget-capped, return an explicit unavailable/capacity response.
```

### 10.2 Initial timeout model

Use separate timeouts for separate failure modes.

Recommended starting configuration, to be benchmark-tuned:

```yaml
routing:
  local_queue_wait_seconds: 120
  local_connection_timeout_seconds: 15
  local_first_token_timeout_seconds: 300
  local_total_request_timeout_seconds: 1200
  cloud_fallback_on_queue_timeout: true
  cloud_fallback_on_slow_ttft_alone: false
  cloud_fallback_on_connection_failure: true
  cloud_fallback_on_local_5xx: policy_controlled
```

The values are intentionally generous because the objective is to use local inference when it is functioning, not optimize for minimum latency at any cost.

Per-model values should replace these defaults once sufficient history exists.

### 10.3 Capacity-aware admission

Every deployment stores a qualification envelope:

```text
qualified_max_context_per_request
qualified_concurrency
qualified_aggregate_context
recommended_concurrency
hard_concurrency_limit
KV cache dtype
GPU memory utilization target
preemptio

*(truncated, 53036 chars total)*

#!/usr/bin/env bash

test "$(pvesh get /cluster/nextid)" = "$VMID"

curl -fsS https://cloud-images.ubuntu.com/noble/current/SHA256SUMS -o SHA256SUMS

test ! -e /var/lib/vz/snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml
cat > /var/lib/vz/snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml <<'CLOUD'
#cloud-config
hostname: marion-ia-usa-llm-manager
manage_etc_hosts: true
disable_root: true
ssh_pwauth: false
package_update: true
packages:
  - qemu-guest-agent
  - python3-venv
  - nginx
  - curl
  - ca-certificates
runcmd:

CLOUD

qm set "$VMID" --efidisk0 local-lvm:0,efitype=4m,pre-enrolled-keys=1
qm set "$VMID" --scsi0 "local-lvm:0,import-from=$IMAGE,discard=on,ssd=1"
qm resize "$VMID" scsi0 64G
qm set "$VMID" --ide2 local-lvm:cloudinit --ipconfig0 ip=dhcp --cicustom user=local:snippets/llm-manager-114.yaml --boot order=scsi0
qm set "$VMID" --description 'LLM Manager shell. Created fresh 2026-09-08. DHCP bootstrap; static DNS pending. Source: HANDOFF.md in Codex LLM manager VM creation workspace. No GPU passthrough. Do not manage VM107 or CT906.'

## 6. Scoped future work: 100 Gb ring (do LAST, per Jordan)

Targets: **miam-00111, miam-00143, miam-00144** — port-2 PCIe 100 Gb NICs. MIAM-00112 excluded (no 100 Gb path documented).
- **RDMA-0 (read-only inventory, plan §19.3):** on each host AND inside each inference VM: `lspci -nn; lspci -t; ethtool -i <nic>; ethtool <nic>; ip -br link; rdma link; rdma dev; ibv_devinfo; nvidia-smi topo -m` — record NIC model/BDF/driver/firmware, link peers, MTU, IP, IOMMU group, GPU↔NIC topology, current VM passthrough.
- **RDMA-1:** per-link iperf3 TCP + RDMA verbs tests (directional — ring can be asymmetric).
- **RDMA-2:** NCCL transport proof — want `NET/IB/GDRDMA`; if logs show socket transport, record explicitly. Host RDMA ≠ VM RDMA: guest needs PCI passthrough of a dedicated 100 Gb port or validated SR-IOV VF design.
- **RDMA-3/4:** small multi-node vLLM functional test → >120 GB model qualification. Multi-node deployment = ONE logical deployment with multiple hosts. Production routing only after plan §19.8 gates.

## 7. Pitfalls learned (hard-won — read before operating)

- **cloud-init noble re-runs only on new instance-id.** To change a booted VM's identity/IP: stop → edit snippets (bump instance-id vN→vN+1) → `qm set --ipconfig0 ...` → start. Patching after boot does nothing.
- **pexpect SSH:** after sending password, expect only `EOF`/`TIMEOUT`; treat `Permission denied` as the ONLY auth failure (qm config prints literal `cipassword: ****` that false-matches). Batch all remote commands into ONE ssh call (base64 script) — hosts rate-limit rapid reconnects. Use `ssh_run.py`.
- **`qm guest exec` must run on the node owning the VM** (VM103 commands go through node 111, not 135 — you'll get "config file does not exist" otherwise). Same for `pct exec/push` (CT115 → node 147).
- **Debian 12 LXCs have no sudo** — use `runuser -u postgres -- psql`.
- **OPNsense SSH (10.0.20.1:22) unreachable from LAN** — don't waste time; try HTTPS API instead.
- **LLDAP SSH rejects password but HTTP API accepts it** — do all LLDAP work via GraphQL.
- **vmbr0 HAS DHCP** (contrary to old skill notes) — VM114 held lease .207 before static config.
- **.161 vs .162 model-len mismatch:** 262144 vs 32768 — don't assume homogeneous deployments.
- **Locale warnings in Debian CTs are harmless noise.**
- **10.0.20.114 = physical node miam-00114** — never assign to a guest.

## 8. Constraints / no-touch list

- **Never touch:** VM108 (Biraj), VM107 (GDI controller, other agent), CT900 (template), CT906 (this agent's Hermes), any writes to MIAM-00100 (data preservation).
- MIAM-00112: prior agent's work done, mutations permitted — but verify current state before touching; VM401 is serving production-ish traffic.
- No plaintext secrets in files/repos/history. No prompt-content logging by default. No OpenRouter without spend caps. No unqualified deployment in routing. No PDU power control. (Full rules: plan §23.)
- Git boundary: strategy docs may go to `jordatech` repos on branch `AGENT_STEA004_ENTREPRENEUR` if Jordan wants them versioned — ask first.
